 2. Which of the following components is generally seen outside the Central Processing Unit (CPU) of
    a computer?
    my; oelafjk Wmdx. w;=frka mß.Klhl uOHu ieliqï tallhg (CPU) msg;ska idudkHfhka oelsh
    yelafla l=ula o?

    1)   RAM
    2)   Control Unit / md,k tallh
    3)   ALU
    4)   4) General purpose registers / fmdÿld¾h frðia;r
    5)   L1 cache memory / L1 ksys; u;lh

                                                                                                 ( 2017 – 02 )
    Answer : 1 )
    Explanation / meyeÈ,s lsÍu
    Option/ úl,amh 1)
    RAM (Random Access Memory) is a main memory component located outside the CPU on the computer's
    motherboard. It stores data and instructions temporarily while the CPU processes them. Although closely
    connected to the CPU, it is not part of the CPU chip itself.
    RAM ^iiïNdù m%fõY u;lh& hkq mß.Klfha ujq mqjrefõ CPU j,ska msg; msysgd we;s m%Odk
    u;l ixrplhls' th CPU tAjd ilik w;r;=r ;djld,slj o;a; iy Wmfoia .nvd lrhs' CPU
    iuÕ iómj iïnkaO jqjo" th CPU Ñmfhau fldgila fkdfõ'

    Option/ úl,amh 2)
    The Control Unit is a fundamental part of the CPU. It manages and coordinates the activities of all
    computer parts by directing the flow of instructions and data. It resides inside the CPU and works closely
    with the ALU and registers.
    md,k tAllh CPU ys uQ,sl fldgils' th Wmfoia iy o;a; m%jdyh fufyhùfuka ish¨u mß.Kl
    fldgiaj, l%shdldrlï l<ukdlrKh lr iïnkaëlrKh lrhs' th CPU ;=< msysgd we;s w;r ALU
    iy frcsia;r iuÕ iómj l%shd lrhs'

    Option/ úl,amh 3)
    The ALU performs all arithmetic and logic operations such as addition, subtraction, and comparisons. It
    is an internal CPU component that works directly with the Control Unit and registers to execute
    instructions.
    ALU tl;= lsÍu" wvq lsÍu iy iei£ï jeks ish¨u wxl .Ks;uh iy ;d¾lsl fufyhqï isÿ lrhs'
    th md,k tAllh iuÕ iDcqju l%shd lrk iy Wmfoia l%shd;aul lsÍug ,shdmÈxÑ jk wNHka;r
    CPU ixrplhls'

    Option/ úl,amh 4)
    These are small, fast memory locations inside the CPU used to temporarily hold data during processing.
    Registers store intermediate results, memory addresses, and instruction data during CPU operations. They
    are not accessible from outside the CPU.
    fïjd ieliSfï§ o;a; ;djld,slj r|jd .ekSug Ndú;d lrk CPU ;=< l=vd" fõ.j;a u;l ia:dk fõ'
    CPU fufyhqï w;r;=r w;rueÈ m%;sM," u;l ,smsk iy Wmfoia o;a; frðiag¾ .nvd lrhs' tAjd
    CPU j,ska msg; isg m%fõY úh fkdyel'

    Option/ úl,amh 5)
    The L1 cache is the fastest and smallest cache memory, located inside the CPU chip. It stores frequently
    accessed data and instructions to speed up processing. It is closer to the CPU core than other cache levels
    like L2 or L3.
    L1 ksys;a u;lh hkq CPU Ñmh ;=< msysgd we;s fõ.j;au iy l=vdu ksys; u;lhhs' th ieliSu
    fõ.j;a lsÍu i|yd ks;r m%fõY jk o;a; iy Wmfoia .nvd lrhs' th L2 fyda L3 jeks wfkl=;a
    ksys; uÜgï j,g jvd CPU yrhg ióm fõ'

    Therefore, option 1) is the correct answer.
    tneúka" 1) úl,amh ksjerÈ ms<s;=r fõ'

 3. Which of the following secondary storage devices is generally considered to provide the fastest access
    to data?
    my; i|yka oaú;Shsl wdphk Wml%u we;=frka fõ.j;a u o;a; m%fõYh ,nd fok Wml%uh f,i
    idudkHfhka i,lkq ,nkafka l=ula o?
    1) Compact Disc / iqixys; ;eáh
    2) Digital Versatile Disc / wxls; nyq ksmqk ;eáh
    3) Internal hard disk / wNHka;r oDV äialh
    4) Magnetic tape / pqïnls; máh
    5) Floppy disk / kuH äialh

                                                                                                     ( 2017 – 05 )
    Answer : 3 )
    Explanation / meyeÈ,s lsÍu
    Option/ úl,amh 1)
    A Compact Disc is an optical storage medium that uses a laser to read and write data. Access speed is
    significantly slower than hard drives due to the mechanical movement and lower data transfer rates. CDs
    are mostly used for media storage and data backups.
    ixhqla; ;eáhla hkq o;a; lshùug iy ,sùug f,ai¾ Ndú;d lrk oDYH .nvd udOHhls' hdka;%sl
    p,kh iy wvq o;a; yqjudre wkqmd; fya;=fjka m%fõY fõ.h oDV ;eá j,g jvd ie,lsh hq;= f,i
    ukao.dó fõ' CD ;eá fndfyda ÿrg udOH .nvd lsÍu iy o;a; Wmia: i|yd Ndú;d fõ'
    Option/ úl,amh 2)
    A DVD is similar to a CD but with higher capacity. Like CDs, it is an optical device and is slower than a
    hard disk in terms of read/write speed. Although DVDs can store more data than CDs, their access time is
    still not competitive with hard drives.
    DVD ;eáhla CD tllg iudk kuq;a by< Odß;djla we;' CD fuka" th oDYH Wmdx.hla jk w;r
    lshùfï$,sùfï fõ.h wkqj oDV ;eáhlg jvd ukao.dó fõ' DVD j,g CD j,g jvd jeä o;a;
    .nvd l< yels jqjo" tAjdfha m%fõY ld,h ;ju;a oDV ;eá iuÕ ;rÕldÍ fkdfõ'
    Option/ úl,amh 3)
    An internal hard disk (HDD) is a magnetic storage device installed inside the computer. It provides
    relatively fast access to data compared to other secondary storage devices like optical discs or tapes. While
    SSDs (solid-state drives) are faster, among the options listed, HDDs offer the best performance in terms
    of speed and data access time.
    wNHka;r oDV ;eáhla (HDD) hkq mß.Klh ;=< ia:dmkh lr we;s pqïNl .nvd Wmdx.hls' oDYH
    ;eá fyda má jeks wfkl=;a oaú;shsl .nvd Wmdx. yd ii|k úg th o;a; j,g idfmalaIj fõ.j;a
    m%fõYhla imhhs' SSD (>k-;;aj Odjl) fõ.j;a jqjo" ,ehsia;=.; lr we;s úl,am w;r" HDDs
    fõ.h iy o;a; m%fõY ld,h wkqj fyd|u ld¾h idOkh ,nd fohs'
    Option/ úl,amh 4)
    Magnetic tape is a very old storage medium used primarily for backups and archival purposes. It offers
    high capacity but very slow access times since data is read sequentially. It's not suitable for fast or random-
    access data retrieval.
    pqïnl má hkq m%Odk jYfhka Wmia: iy f,aLkd.dr wruqKq i|yd Ndú;d lrk b;d merKs .nvd
    udOHhls' th by< Odß;djla ,nd fok kuq;a o;a; wkqms<sfj,ska lshjk neúka b;d ukao.dó m%fõY
    fõ,djka ,nd fohs' th fõ.j;a fyda wyUq-m%fõY o;a; ,nd .ekSu i|yd iqÿiq fkdfõ'
    Option/ úl,amh 5)
    Floppy disks are outdated and offer very low capacity and extremely slow access speed compared to
    modern storage solutions. They were used mainly in the 1980s and 1990s and are no longer practical for
    modern computing needs.
    kuH ;eá h,a mek .sh tAjd jk w;r kùk .nvd úi÷ï yd ii|k úg b;d wvq Odß;djla iy
    w;sYh ukao.dó m%fõY fõ.hla ,nd fohs' tAjd m%Odk jYfhka 1980 iy 1990 .Kkaj, Ndú;d lrk
    ,o w;r kùk mß.Kl wjYH;d i|yd ;jÿrg;a m%dfhda.sl fkdfõ'

    Therefore, option 3) is the correct answer.
    tneúka" 3) úl,amh ksjerÈ ms<s;=r fõ'

 4. Which of the followings is a main use of Complementary Metal-Oxide Semiconductor (CMOS)
    memory of a personal computer?
    mqoa., mß.Klhl wkqmQrl f,day Tlaihsv w¾O ikakdhl u;lfha (CMOS) m%Odk Ndú;hla jkafka
    my; oelafjk oE w;=frka l=ula o?
    1) Keeping inputs for processing
       ieliSu i|yd wdodk ;nd .ekSu
    2) Holding instructions for operations
       fufyhqï i|yd Wmfoia r|jd .ekSu
    3) Providing space for loading operating system
       fufyhqï moaO;sh m%fõYkh i|yd wjldY ,nd§u
    4) Retaining information for output
       m%;sodkh i|yd f;dr;=re ;nd .ekSu
    5) Keeping Basic Input Output System settings for the booting procedure
       n, .ekaùfï l%shdj,sh i|yd uQ,sl wdodk m%;sodk moaO;sfha lÜg,a wx. ;nd .ekSu

                                                                                                ( 2017 – 07 )
    Answer : 5 )
    The main use of Complementary Metal-Oxide Semiconductor (CMOS) memory in a personal computer
    is to keep the Basic Input Output System (BIOS) settings used during the booting procedure. CMOS stores
    important configuration data such as system time and date, hardware settings, and the boot sequence. This
    information is essential for the BIOS to properly initialize the system hardware when the computer is
    turned on. Unlike RAM, CMOS retains its data even when the computer is powered off, thanks to a small
    battery on the motherboard. The other options listed—such as keeping inputs, holding operational
    instructions, or loading the operating system—are tasks handled by other components like RAM or the
    hard drive, not the CMOS. Therefore, the correct and specific function of CMOS memory is to store BIOS
    settings needed for the computer to boot correctly.
    mqoa.,sl mß.Klhl wkqmQrl f,day - Tlaihsv w¾O ikakdhl (CMOS) u;lfha m%Odk Ndú;h jkafka
    n, .ekaùfï l%shdj,sh i|yd uQ,sl wdodk m%;sodk moaO;sfha (Basic Input Output System) lÜg,a wx.
    (settings) ;nd .ekSuhs' CMOS uÕska moaO;s fõ,dj iy Èkh" oDvdx. ieliqï iy werUqï wkqms<sfj,
    jeks jeo.;a úkHdi o;a; .nvd lrhs' mß.Klh il%sh lr we;s úg moaO;s oDvdx. ksis f,i wdrïN
    lsÍug BIOS yg fuu f;dr;=re w;HjYH fõ' RAM fuka fkdj" ujq mqjrefõ l=vd negßhla ksid
    mß.Klh l%shd úrys; jQ úg mjd CMOS tys o;a; r|jd .kS' ,ehsia;=.; lr we;s wfkl=;a úl,am
    - ieliSu i|yd wdodk ;nd .ekSu" fufyhqï i|yd Wmfoia r|jd .ekSu" fufyhqï moaO;sh m%fõYkh
    (loading) i|yd wjldY ,nd §u fyda m%;sodkh i|yd f;dr;=re ;nd .ekSu - CMOS fkdj RAM
    fyda oDV ;eáh jeks wfkl=;a ixrpl u.ska yiqrejk ld¾hhka fõ' tneúka" CMOS u;lfha ksjerÈ
    iy ksYaÑ; ld¾hh jkafka mß.Klh ksjerÈj wdrïN lsÍug wjYH BIOS ieliqï .nvd lsÍuhs'

    Therefore, option 5) is the correct answer.
    tneúka" 5) úl,amh ksjerÈ ms<s;=r fõ'

 5. A …… on motherboard is used to expand the functionality of a computer.
    uõ mqjrej u; we;s …… mß.Klhl l%shdldß;ajh úoeySu (expand) i|yd Ndú; lrhs'
    Which of the followings is the most appropriate to fill in the blank in the above statement?
    by; j.ka;sfha ysia;ek msrùu i|yd jvd;a iqÿiq jkafka my; i|yka ljrla o?

    1) Bus              2) Clock            3) RAM           4) Slot            5) ROM
       nih                 Trf,daiqj                            újrh

                                                                                               ( 2017 – 12 )
    Answer : 4 )
    The most appropriate word to fill in the blank is "slot", making the complete sentence.
    ysia;ek msrùu i|yd jvd;a iqÿiq jpkh újrh jk w;r th jdlHh iïmQ¾K lrhs'

    "A slot on motherboard is used to expand the functionality of a computer."
    uõ mqjrej u; we;s újrh mß.Klhl l%shdldß;ajh úoeySu (expand) i|yd Ndú; lrhs'

    Slots on the motherboard, such as PCI (Peripheral Component Interconnect) or RAM slots, are used to
    add expansion cards or modules like graphics cards, sound cards, network cards, or additional memory.
    These components enhance the computer's capabilities beyond its basic configuration. In contrast, a bus
    is a communication system for transferring data, a clock keeps timing signals, RAM is used for temporary
    data storage, and ROM holds permanent instructions like firmware. None of these directly serve the
    purpose of expanding hardware functionality in the way slots do. Therefore, slot is the most accurate
    answer.
    PCI ^m¾hka; ixrpl wka;¾ iïnkaOlh& fyda RAM ;õ jeks ujq mqjrefõ ;õ" .%e*sla ldâm;a" Yío
    ldâm;a" cd, ldâm;a fyda wu;r u;lh jeks mq¿,a lsÍfï ldâm;a fyda fudähq, tl;= lsÍug Ndú;d
    lrhs' fuu ixrpl mß.Klfha uQ,sl úkHdifhka Tíng yelshdjka jeä ÈhqKq lrhs' Bg fjkiaj"
    nihla hkq o;a; udre lsÍu i|yd ikaksfõok moaO;shls" Trf,daiqjla ld, ix{d ;nd .kS" RAM
    ;djld,sl o;a; .nvd lsÍu i|yd Ndú;d lrhs" iy ROM iaÓrdx. jeks iaÓr Wmfoia orhs' fï
    lsisjla újrh lrk wdldrhg oDvdx. l%shdldß;ajh mq¿,a lsÍfï wruqKg iDcqju fiajh fkdlrhs'
    tneúka" újrh hkq jvd;a ksjerÈ ms<s;=rhs'

    Therefore, option 4) is the correct answer.
    tneúka" 4) úl,amh ksjerÈ ms<s;=r fõ'

 6. Consider the following data input devices:
    my; oelafjk o;a; wdodk Wmdx. i,lkak(
    A - On screen keyboard / ;srh u; oelafjk h;=re mqjrej (virtual keyboard)
    B - Bar code reader / ;Sre fla; lshjkh
    C - Magnetic card reader / pqïnl ldâ m;a lshjkh
    Which of the above devices can be used to input data more efficiently?
    jvd;a ld¾hlaIuj o;a; wdodk lsÍu i|yd by; ljr Wmdx. Ndú; l< yels o?

    1) A only.           2) B only.           3) C only.           4) A and B only.     5) B and C only.
       A muKs.              B muKs.              C muKs.              A yd B muKs.         B yd C muKs.

                                                                                                   ( 2017 – 39 )
    Answer : 5 )
    Explanation / meyeÈ,s lsÍu
    Let’s define the devices. / Wmdx. y÷kd .ksuq'
    Statement / m%ldYh A)
    An on-screen keyboard is a virtual input device displayed on a computer or mobile screen, allowing users
    to enter text by tapping characters. While useful in touchscreen devices or for accessibility, it is not
    efficient for high-speed data entry. Typing on a screen is slower, more error-prone, and requires continuous
    manual effort, making it unsuitable for tasks that demand quick or repetitive input.
    ;srh u; h;=re mqjrejla hkq mß.Klhl fyda cx.u ;srhl o¾Ykh jk w;:H wdodk Wmdx.hla
    jk w;r" mßYS,lhskag wlaIr tap lsÍfuka mdG we;=<;a lsÍug bv i,ihs' iam¾Y ;sr Wmdx.j,
    fyda m%fõYH;dj i|yd m%fhdackj;a jqjo" th wêfõ.S o;a; we;=<;a lsÍu i|yd ld¾hlaIu fkdfõ'
    ;srhla u; ghsma lsÍu ukao.dó" jvd;a fodaI iys; jk w;r wLKav w;ska W;aidyhla wjYH jk
    w;r tuÕska blauka fyda mqkrdj¾;k wdodkhla wjYH jk ld¾hhka i|yd th kqiqÿiq fõ'

    Statement / m%ldYh B)
    A bar code reader is a highly efficient input device that scans barcodes and instantly converts them into
    digital data. It eliminates manual entry, reducing errors and significantly speeding up the process of data
    capture. Barcode readers are commonly used in retail, inventory management, and logistics, where speed
    and accuracy are crucial for handling large volumes of items.
    ;Sre fla; lshjkh hkq ;Sre fla; mßf,dalkh lr tAjd laIKslj äðg,a o;a; njg mßj¾;kh lrk
    b;d ld¾hlaIu wdodk Wmdx.hls' th w;ska we;=<;a lsÍu bj;a lrhs" fodaI wvq lrhs iy o;a;
    .%yKh lsÍfï l%shdj,sh ie,lsh hq;= f,i fõ.j;a lrhs' ;Sre fla; lshjkhka nyq,j Ndú;d jkafka
    is,a,r" bkafjkagß l<ukdlrKh iy iemhqï lghq;=j, jk w;r" tys§ úYd, whs;u m%udKhla
    yeisrùu i|yd fõ.h iy ksrjoH;djh b;d jeo.;a fõ'

    Statement / m%ldYh C)
    A magnetic card reader is an input device that reads data stored in the magnetic stripe of cards such as
    credit cards or ID badges. With a simple swipe, the reader quickly retrieves and inputs data into the system,
    making it a fast and reliable solution for environments like banking, access control, and point-of-sale
    systems. Its automation and speed make it far more efficient than manual input methods.

 2. Which of the following correctly lists the given computer storage components in the descending
    order of access speed?
    § we;s mß.Kl wdphk wx. m%fõY fõ.fhys (access speed) wjfrdayK ms<sfj<g ksjerÈj fm<.iajd
    we;af;a my; ljrl o?
    1) cache memory > main memory > magnetic disk > register
       ksys; u;lh > m%Odk u;lh > pqïNl äialh > frðia;rh
    2) magnetic disk > main memory > cache memory > register
       pqïNl äialh > m%Odk u;lh > ksys; u;lh > frðia;rh
    3) magnetic disk > main memory > register > cache memory
       pqïNl äialh > m%Odk u;lh > frðia;rh > ksys; u;lh
    4) register > cache memory > main memory > magnetic disk
       frðia;rh > ksys; u;lh > m%Odk u;lh > pqïNl äialh
    5) register > main memory > magnetic disk > cache memory
       frðia;rh > m%Odk u;lh > pqïNl äialh > ksys; u;lh

                                                                                                     ( 2018 – 12 )
    Answer : 4 )

    This question is about arranging computer storage components in descending order of access speed. The
    correct order is register > cache memory > main memory > magnetic disk, as given in option 4.
    fuu m%Yakh mß.Kl .nvd ixrpl m%fõY fõ.fha wjfrdayK wkqms<sfj,g ieliSu .ek h' ksjerÈ
    wkqms<sfj, jkafka úl,am 4 ys olajd we;s mßÈ frðia;rh > ksys; u;lh > m%Odk u;lh > pqïNl
    äialh fõ'

    Register / frðia;rh
    Registers are the fastest storage components in a computer, as they are located inside the CPU and are
    directly accessed during processing.
    frðia;rh hkq mß.Klhl fõ.j;au .nvd ixrpl fõ" ukao tAjd CPU ;=< msysgd we;s w;r
    ieliSfï§ iDcqju m%fõY fõ'

    Cache Memory / ksys; u;lh
    Cache is slightly slower than registers but still very fast because it stores frequently accessed data close to
    the CPU.
    ksys;h frðia;r j,g jvd ;rula ukao.dó kuq;a ;ju;a b;d fõ.j;a jkafka th ks;r m%fõY jk
    o;a; CPU wi, .nvd lrk neúks'

    Main Memory (RAM) / m%Odk u;lh
    Main memory is slower than cache but faster than secondary storage like magnetic disks. It stores data
    that the CPU needs during active operations.
    m%Odk u;lh ksys;hg jvd ukao.dó kuq;a pqïnl ;eá jeks oaú;shsl .nvdjg jvd fõ.j;ah' l%shdldÍ
    fufyhqï j,§ CPU g wjYH o;a; th .nvd lrhs'

    Magnetic Disk / pqïNl äialh
    This is the slowest among the listed components as it relies on mechanical movements to read/write data.
    o;a; lshùug$,sùug hdka;%sl p,khka u; r|d mj;sk neúka ,ehsia;=.; lr we;s ixrpl w;ßka
    fuh ukao.dóu fõ'
    The descending order in option 4 reflects this hierarchy, which is why it’s the correct answer.
    úl,am 4 ys wjfrdayK wkqms<sfj, fuu Oqrdj,sh ms<sìUq lrhs" tneúka th ksjerÈ ms<s;=r fõ'

 3. Consider the following computer memory types.
    my; oelafjk mß.Kl u;l j¾. i,lkak'

    A - CMOS memory / CMOS u;lh
    B - cache memory / ksys; u;lh
    C - flash memory / iefk,s u;lh
    D - hard disk / oDV ;eáh
    E - RAM
    F – registers / frðia;r

    Which of the above are volatile memory types?
    by; oE w;=frka kIH (volatile) u;l j¾. jkafka:

    1) A, C and D only                 2) A, D and E only                 3) A, E and F only
       A, C iy D muKs                     A, D iy E muKs                     A, E iy F muKs

    4) B, E and F only                 5) C, E and F only
       B, E iy F muKs                     C, E iy F muKs

                                                                                                  ( 2018 – 13 )
    Answer : 4 )

    This question focuses on identifying volatile memory types, which lose their stored data when the power
    supply is turned off. / fuu m%Yakh wjOdkh fhduq lrkafka n, iemhqu wl%sh l< úg .nvd lr we;s
    o;a; ke;s lrk kIH u;l j¾. y÷kd .ekSu flfrys h'

    CMOS Memory / CMOS u;lh
    This is not volatile because it retains settings like system configuration even when powered off, as it uses
    a small battery. / fuh kIH fkdfõ" ukao th l=vd negßhla Ndú;d lrk neúka th l%shd úrys; l<
    úg mjd moaO;s úkHdih jeks ieliqï r|jd .kS'

    Cache Memory / ksys; u;lh
    This is volatile, as it is a high-speed memory that stores frequently accessed data temporarily for quick
    access by the CPU. / fuh kIH fõ" ukao th CPU u.ska blauka m%fõYh i|yd ;djld,slj ks;r
    m%fõY jk o;a; .nvd lrk wêfõ.S u;lhls'

    Flash Memory / iefk,s u;lh
    This is non-volatile, meaning it retains data without power (commonly used in USB drives and SSDs).
    fuh kIH fkdfõ" tkï th n,h fkdue;sj o;a; r|jd .kS (commonly used in USB drives and SSD)
    j, nyq,j Ndú;d fõ'

    Hard Disk / oDV ;eáh
    This is non-volatile storage that persists data permanently, even without power.
    fuh kIH fkdjk .nvdjla jk w;r th n,h fkdue;sj jqjo iaÓrju o;a; mj;S'

    RAM
    This is volatile, and its data is erased when the computer is powered off.
    fuh kIH jk w;r" mß.Klh l%shd úrys; l< úg tys o;a; uld oukq ,efí'

    Register / frðia;r
    Registers are volatile as they temporarily store data within the CPU during processing and lose this data
    when power is lost. / ieliqï w;r;=r CPU ;=< ;djld,slj o;a; .nvd lrk w;r úÿ,sh úikaê jQ
    úg fuu o;a; ke;s jk neúka frðiag¾ kIH fõ'

    Option 4 is correct because cache memory, RAM and registers are all volatile memory types. The
    other options include non-volatile memory types, making them incorrect.
    ksys; u;lh, RAM iy frðia;r ish,a,u kIH u;l j¾. jk neúka 4 jk úl,amh ksjerÈhs'
    wfkl=;a úl,am w;r kIH fkdjk u;l j¾. we;=<;a jk w;r tAjd jerÈ lrhs'

 4. Consider the following statements related to the development of computers over time.
    ld,h;a iu. mß.Klj, ixj¾Okhg wod< my; m%ldY i,lkak'
    A. Both processing speed and power consumption of computers have increased.

    Statement / m%ldYh A)
    This statement is Incorrect. / fuu m%ldYh jerÈhs'

    improved clock speeds).

    fõ.h jeä ù we;'

    Power consumption has decreased, not increased, due to more energy-efficient hardware, such as low-
    power CPUs and improved semiconductor technologies.
    wvq n,e;s CPU iy jeäÈhqKq l< w¾O ikakdhl ;dlaIKhka jeks jvd;a n,Yla;s ld¾hlaIu oDvdx.
    ksid n, mßfNdackh wvq ù we;" jeä ù ke;'

    laptops.

    ùug ks¾udKh lr we;'

    Statement / m%ldYh B)
    This statement is Correct. / fuu m%ldYh ksjerÈhs'
    Computers have become faster due to advancements in microprocessors and solid-state storage.
    laIqø ilikhka iy >k-;;aj .nvd lsÍfï ÈhqKqj ksid mß.Kl fõ.j;a ù we;'

    At the same time, computers have become smaller, evolving from room-sized mainframes to compact
    laptops and smartphones.
    tA iuÕu" ldur m%udKfha m%Odk rduq j,ska ixhqla; ,emafgdma iy iaud¾Üf*daka olajd mßKduh fjñka
    mß.Kl l=vd ù we;'

    Statement / m%ldYh C)
    This statement is Correct. / fuu m%ldYh ksjerÈhs'
    Power consumption has reduced due to the development of energy-efficient processors (e.g., ARM
    processors in mobile devices).
    n,Yla;s ld¾hlaIu ilikhka ^Wod( cx.u Wmdx.j, ARM ilikhka& ixj¾Okh ùu ksid n,
    mßfNdackh wvq ù we;'

    Physical size has decreased significantly with innovations like nanotechnology, integrated circuits (IC),
    and compact storage solutions.
    kefkda ;dlaIKh" tAldnoaO mßm: (IC) iy ixhqla; .nvd úi÷ï jeks kfjda;amdokhka iuÕ fN!;sl
    m%udKh ie,lsh hq;= f,i wvq ù we;'

 2. In the memory hierarchy diagram given, which of the following represents A, B, and C respectively?
    § we;s u;l OQrdj,s igykg wkqj my; ljrla A, B, iy C ms<sfj<ska ksrEmkh lrhs o?

       pqïnl máh" pqïLl (oDV) ;eáh" iiïNdú m%fõY u;lh (RAM)" ilikfhys frðia;r
    2) Processor Registers. Magnetic (Hard) Disk, Random Access Memory (RAM), Magnetic Tape
       ilikfhys frðia;r" jqïnl (oDV) ;eáh" iiïNdù m%fõY u;lh" pqïnl máh
    3) Processor Registers, Random Access Memory (RAM), Magnetic (Hard) Disk, Magnetic Tape
       ilikfhys frðia;r" iiïNdù m%fõY u;lh" jqïnl (oDV) ;eáh" pqïnl máh
    4) Processor Registers, Random Access Memory (RAM), Magnetic Tape, Magnetic (Hard) Disk
       ilikfhys frðia;r" iiïNdù m%fõY u;lh" jqïnl máh" pqïnl (oDV) ;eáh
    5) Random Access Memory (RAM), Processor Registers, Magnetic (Hard) Disk, Magnetic Tape
       iiïNdú m%fõY u;lh" ilikfhys frÊia;r" pqïnl (oDV) ;eáh" jqïnl máh

                                                                                              ( 2019 – 19 )
    Answer : 3 )
    Explanation / meyeÈ,s lsÍu

    The memory hierarchy is structured based on speed, cost, and storage capacity.
    u;l OQrdj,sh jHqy.; ù we;af;a fõ.h" msßjeh iy .nvd Odß;dj u; mokïjh'

    A - Processor Registers / ilikfhys frðia;r
    These are the fastest and smallest memory locations inside the CPU, used for immediate data processing.
    fïjd laIKsl o;a; ieliSu i|yd Ndú;d lrk CPU ;=< we;s fõ.j;au iy l=vdu u;l ia:dk fõ'

    B - Random Access Memory (RAM) / iiïNdú m%fõY u;lh
    This is the main volatile memory used for temporary data storage while programs are running. It is much
    slower than registers but faster than secondary storage.
    jevigyka l%shd;aul jk úg ;djld,sl o;a; .nvd lsÍu i|yd Ndú;d lrk m%Odk kYH u;lh
    fuhhs' th frðiag¾ j,g jvd fndfyda fiñka kuq;a oaú;shsl .nvdjg jvd fõ.j;a fõ'

    C - Magnetic (Hard) Disk / pqïnl (oDV) ;eáh
    Hard drives are non-volatile storage used for long-term data storage. They are slower than RAM but
    provide larger storage capacity.
    oDV ;eá È.=ld,Sk o;a; .nvd lsÍu i|yd Ndú;d lrk kYH fkdjk .nvd fõ' tAjd RAM j,g
    jvd ukao.dó kuq;a úYd, .nvd Odß;djla imhhs'

    D - Magnetic Tape/ jqïnl máh
    Magnetic tapes are the slowest form of storage, typically used for backups and archival purposes.
    pqïnl má hkq .nvd lsÍfï ukao.dó wdldrhhs" idudkHfhka Wmia: iy f,aLkd.dr wruqKq i|yd
    Ndú;d fõ'

     2020 CONCEPT OF COMPUTER MCQ MARKING

   1. A computer processor will operate fastest when the data that it wants is in the
      mß.Klhl ilikh jvd;au fõ.fhka fufyhjkq ,nkafka thg wjYH o;a; my; ljrl we;s úgo?

          1) cache memory 2) hard disk                                    3) magnetic tape                  4) main memory 5) optical disk
             ksys; u;lfhys   oDV ;eáfhys                                     pqïnl máfhys                      m%Odk u;lfhys  m%ldY ;eáfhys

                                                                                                                                                         ( 2020 – 01 )
          Answer : 1 )
          When a processor (CPU) needs data, it tries to retrieve it from different levels of memory based on speed
          and accessibility
          ilikhg (CPU) o;a; wjYH jQ úg" th fõ.h iy m%fõYH;dj u; mokïj úúO u;l uÜgïj,ska
          th ,nd .ekSug W;aidy lrhs

          Cache Memory / kYH u;lh
          Cache is the fastest type of memory and sits closest to the CPU. It’s divided into levels (L1, L2, L3), with
          each level representing different physical distances from the processor and different capacities. Data that
          the CPU frequently needs or recently accessed is stored here, allowing CPU to access it almost instantly.
          kYH u;lh hkq fõ.j;au u;l j¾.h jk w;r th CPU tlg wdikakj msysgd we;' th uÜgï
          j,g fnod we; (L1,L2 L3) tla tla uÜgï ilikfha úúO fN!;sl ÿr iy úúO Odß;djka ksfhdackh
          lrhs' CPU yg ks;r wjYH fyda uE;l§ m%fõY jQ o;a; fuys .nvd lr we;s w;r" CPU yg
          laIKslj mdfya m%fõY ùug bv i,ihs'

          Speed and Size / fõ.h iy m%udKh
          Cache memory is very fast, often operating at or near the speed of the CPU itself, which makes it ideal for
          quick data retrieval. However, it is also very limited in size compared to other memory types.
          kYH u;lh b;d fõ.j;a jk w;r" fndfyda úg CPU tflau fyda Bg wdikak fõ.hlska l%shd lrhs"
          th blauka o;a; ,nd .ekSu i|yd jvd;a iqÿiq fõ' flfia fj;;a" wfkl=;a u;l j¾. yd ii|k úg
          th m%udKfhka o b;d iSñ;h'

          Main Memory / m%Odk u;lh (RAM)
          RAM stores data and instructions for programs that are actively running. When the CPU cannot find data
          in cache, it checks the RAM next. RAM is faster than storage drives (like hard disks) but slower than
          cache.
          RAM il%shj l%shd;aul jk jevigyka i|yd o;a; iy Wmfoia .nvd lrhs' CPU yg kYH u;lfha
          o;a; fidhd.; fkdyels jQ úg" th B<Õg RAM mÍlaId lrhs' RAM .nvd Odjl j,g jvd fõ.j;a
          ^oDV ;eá jeks& kuq;a kYHg jvd ukao.dó fõ'

          Speed and Size / fõ.h iy m%udKh
          RAM has a larger capacity than cache, but data retrieval from RAM is slower, as it requires more time for
          the CPU to locate and read the data.
          RAM i;=j kYH u;lhg jvd úYd, Odß;djla we;" kuq;a CPU yg o;a; fidhd .ekSug iy lshùug
          jeä ld,hla wjYH jk neúka RAM fj;ska o;a; ,nd .ekSu ukao.dó fõ'

          Hard Disk / oDV ;eáh
          Hard disks store data permanently and retain it even when the computer is turned off. However, their data
          retrieval is much slower because they involve mechanical movements (in the case of HDDs) or read/write
          processes (in SSDs).
          oDV ;eá iaÓrju o;a; .nvd lr mß.Klh l%shd úrys; l< úg mjd th r|jd ;nd .kS' flfia
          fj;;a" tAjdfha hdka;%sl p,khka (HDDs iïnkaOfhka) fyda lshùfï$,sùfï l%shdj,Ska SSD j, we;=<;a
          jk ksid tAjdfha o;a; ,nd.ekSu b;d ukao.dó fõ'

    Speed and Size / fõ.h iy m%udKh
    Hard disks, especially traditional HDDs, are significantly slower than both RAM and cache. While SSDs
    are faster than HDDs, they still can’t match the speed of RAM or cache for direct CPU access.
    oDV ;eá" úfYaIfhkau idïm%odhsl HDDs ,RAM iy cache hk folgu jvd ie,lsh hq;= f,i ukao.dó

    u;lfha fõ.hg fkd.e,fma'

    Optical Disk and Magnetic Tape / m%ldY ;eá iy pqïnl má
    These are typically used for storage and backup rather than for quick data retrieval. Optical disks
    (CDs/DVDs) use laser technology, while magnetic tape relies on magnetic recording. Both are too slow
    to provide the processor with data at the required speed for active computing.
    fïjd idudkHfhka blaukska o;a; ,nd .ekSu i|yd fkdj .nvd lsÍu iy Wmia: lsÍu i|yd Ndú;d
    fõ' m%ldY ;eá (CDs/DVDs) f,ai¾ ;dlaIKh Ndú;d lrk w;r pqïnl má pqïnl má.; lsÍu u;
    r|d mj;S' il%Sh mß.Kkh i|yd wjYH fõ.fhka o;a; ilikhg ,nd §ug folu ukao.dó fõ'

    Speed and Size / fõ.h iy m%udKh
    They are the slowest in this list for accessing data, making them unsuitable for a processor to fetch
    frequently used information.
    o;a; fj; m%fõY ùu i|yd fuu ,ehsia;=fõ we;s ukao.dóu tAjd jk w;r" tAjd ilikhlg ks;r
    Ndú;d jk f;dr;=re ,nd .ekSug kqiqÿiq fõ'

    type

    lrkafka wmg .nvd j¾.h y÷kd .ekSug wjYH jkafka(

    The lowest latency (delay in accessing data) / wvqu m%udoh ^o;a; fj; m%fõY ùfï m%udoh&

    The highest speed, allowing the CPU to fetch data with minimal delay.
    by<u fõ.h" CPU yg wju m%udohlska o;a; ,nd .ekSug bv i,ihs'

    Cache memory fulfills these requirements best. It’s purpose-built to provide high-speed data access for
    the CPU, making it the correct answer to the question.
    kYH u;lh fuu wjYH;d jvd;a fyd¢ka bgq lrhs' th CPU i|yd wêfõ.S o;a; m%fõYh iemhSug
    wruqKska f.dvk.d we;s w;r" th m%Yakhg ksjerÈ ms<s;=r njg m;a lrhs'

    Final Answer /wjidk ms<s;=r(
    The correct answer is (1) Cache memory, as it is specifically designed to maximize the processor's
    efficiency and speed by storing frequently accessed data nearby.
    ksjerÈ ms<s;=r jkafka (1) kYH u;lh' th úfYaIfhka ks¾udKh lr we;af;a ilikfha ld¾hlaIu;dj

 2. Which of the following hardware components will lose data when power to computer is switched off
    mß.Klhlg ,nd § we;s úÿ,s n,h úikaê l< úg my; l=uk oDVdx. ixrplj, we;s o;a; wysñ
    fõo?
    A. Registers / frðia;r
    B. cache memory / ksys; u;l
    C. main memory / m%Odk u;lh

    1) A only.          2) A and B only.     3) A and C only.     4) B and C only.    5) All of the above.
       A muKs.             A yd B muKs.         A yd C muKs.         B yd C muKs.        by; ish,a,u.

                                                                                                 ( 2020 – 02 )
    Answer : 5 )
    Registers / frðia;r
    Registers are small, high-speed storage locations within the CPU used to store temporary data and
    instructions during processing. They’re fastest form of memory in a computer and are very limited in size.
    frðia;r hkq ieliSfï§ ;djld,sl o;a; iy Wmfoia .nvd lsÍug Ndú;d lrk CPU ;=< l=vd" wêfõ.S
    .nvd ia:dk fõ' tAjd mß.Klhl we;s fõ.j;au u;l wdldrh jk w;r m%udKfhka b;d iSñ;h'
    •   Data Retention / o;a; r|jd ;nd .ekSu
        Registers are volatile, meaning they lose all stored data when the power is turned off. Since registers
        are directly powered by the CPU, any loss of power causes an immediate loss of data.
        frðiag¾ kYH fõ" tkï n,h wl%sh jQ úg .nvd lr we;s ish¨u o;a; ke;s ù hhs' frðiag¾
        iDcqju CPU u.ska n, .ekafjk neúka" ´kEu n,hla wysñ ùu laIKsl o;a; ke;s ùug fya;=fõ'
    Cache Memory / jdrl u;lh
    Cache memory, also volatile, is a small amount of high-speed memory located close to the CPU. It stores
    frequently accessed data and instructions so the CPU can retrieve them quickly.
    jdrl u;lh" kYH fõS" CPU fj; iómj msysgd we;s wêfõ.S u;lfha l=vd m%udKhls' th ks;r
    m%fõY jk o;a; iy Wmfoia .nvd lrhs" túg CPU yg tAjd blaukska ,nd .; yel'
    •   Data Retention / o;a; r|jd ;nd .ekSu
        Cache memory is volatile as well, meaning it too loses all stored data when the power is turned off. It
        relies on continuous power to retain information, just like registers.
        jdrl u;lh o kYH fõS" thska woyia jkafka n,h wl%sh jQ úg .nvd lr we;s ish¨u o;a;
        ke;s ù hk njhs' th frðiag¾ fuka f;dr;=re r|jd .ekSug wLKav n,h u; r|d mj;S'
    Main Memory / m%Odk u;lh (RAM)
    RAM (Random Access Memory), or main memory, temporarily stores data for programs and processes
    actively running on the computer.
    RAM (iiïNdù m%fõY u;lh)" fyda m%Odk u;lh" mß.Klfha l%shdldÍj l%shd;aul jk jevigyka
    iy l%shdj,Ska i|yd o;a; ;djld,slj .nvd lrhs'
    •   Data Retention / o;a; r|jd ;nd .ekSu
        RAM is volatile and loses all stored data when power is turned off. RAM only holds data temporarily
        while the computer is running.
        RAM kYH jk w;r n,h wl%sh jQ úg .nvd lr we;s ish¨u o;a; ke;s fõ' mß.Klh l%shd;aul
        jk úg RAM tl ;djld,slj o;a; r|jd ;nd .kS'
    Final Analysis of Options / úl,am ms<sn| wjidk úYaf,aIKh
    The question asks which components lose data when the computer is turned off. Since all three components
    (registers, cache memory, and main memory) are volatile, they will all lose their data when power is off.
    mß.Klh l%shd úrys; jQ úg o;a; wysñ jk ixrpl fudkjdoehs m%Yakh wihs' ixrpl ;=ku
    ^frðiag¾" jdrl u;lh iy m%Odk u;lh& kYH neúka" n,h wl%sh jQ úg tAjd ish,a,gu o;a; wysñ
    jkq we;'
    Answer / ms<s;=r
    The correct answer is (5) All A, B, and C, as all three components—registers, cache memory, and main
    memory—are volatile and will lose data when power is removed.
    ksjerÈ ms<s;=r (5) ish¨u A,B iy C ixrpl ;=ku - frðiag¾" jdrl u;lh iy m%Odk u;lh kYH
    jk w;r n,h bj;a l< úg o;a; ke;s fõ'

 3. Computer has evolved from the early main frames to the relatively small smart devices with high
    computing power used today. Which of the following inventions contributed to reduce the physical
    size of computers?
    w;S;fha Ndú; jQ úYd, m%udKfha (main frame) mß.Klj, isg j¾;udkfha Ndú; jk by< mß.Kk
    yelshdj we;s m%udKfhka l=vd iqyqre (smart) Wml%u olajd mß.Klh mßKdukh ù we;' my; l=uk
    ksmehqula (invention) mß.Klj, fN!;sl m%udKh wvq lsÍug odhl jQfha o?
    1)   bus / nih
    2)   integrated circuits / wkql,s; mßm:
    3)   registers / frðia;r
    4)   solid state memory / >k ;;a;aj u;l
    5)   vacuum tube / ßla; k<

                                                                                                 ( 2020 – 03 )
    Answer : 2 )

    Bus / nih
    A bus is a communication system that transfers data between different components of a computer (such
    as the CPU, memory, and storage).
    nihla hkq mß.Klhl úúO fldgia (CPU u;lh iy .nvdj jeks& w;r o;a; yqjudre lrk
    ikaksfõok moaO;shls'
    •    Impact on Size / m%udKh u; n,mEu
         The bus system facilitates data transfer but does not directly reduce the physical size of computers.
         nia moaO;sh o;a; yqjudrejg myiqlï imhk kuq;a mß.Klj, fN!;sl m%udKh fl,skau wvq
         fkdlrhs'

    Integrated Circuits / ix.Dys; mßm: (ICs)
    Integrated circuits, also known as microchips, are tiny circuits containing thousands (or even millions) of
    transistors and other components. They replaced larger individual components, allowing much smaller
    and more powerful designs.
    wkql,s; mßm:" uhsfl%dÑma f,io ye¢kafõ" g%dkaisiagr oyia .Kkla ^fyda ñ,shk .Kkla& iy
    wfkl=;a ixrpl wvx.= l=vd mßm: fõ' Tjqka úYd, ;ks ixrpl m%;sia:dmkh lrk ,o w;r" jvd
    l=vd yd jvd n,j;a fudaia;rj,g bv i,ihs'
    •    Impact on Size / m%udKh u; n,mEu
         The invention of integrated circuits was a major breakthrough in reducing the size of computers. By
         consolidating many components onto a single chip, ICs allowed computers to become significantly
         smaller and more efficient.
         wkql,s; mßm: fidhd .ekSu mß.Klj, m%udKh wvq lsÍfï m%Odk fmr<shla úh' fndfyda ixrpl
         ;ks Ñmhla u; tAldnoaO lsÍfuka" ICs mß.Kl ie,lsh hq;= f,i l=vd yd jvd ld¾hlaIu ùug
         bv i,ihs'

    Registers / frðia;r
    Description: Registers are small storage areas within the CPU, used to store temporary data during
    processing.
    frðiag¾ hkq CPU ;=< we;s l=vd .nvd m%foaY" ieliSfï§ ;djld,sl o;a; .nvd lsÍug Ndú;d lrhs'
    •    Impact on Size / m%udKh u; n,mEu
         While registers improve processing speed, they do not significantly affect the physical size of
         computers. Registers are part of the CPU but are not directly related to miniaturization efforts.
         frðiag¾ ieliqï fõ.h jeä ÈhqKq lrk w;r" tAjd mß.Klj, fN!;sl m%udKhg ie,lsh hq;=
         f,i n,mdkafka ke;' frðiag¾ CPU ys fldgila jk kuq;a l=vd lsÍfï W;aidyhka iuÕ iDcqj
         iïnkaO fkdfõ'

    Solid State Memory / >k ;;a;aj u;l
    Solid state memory refers to storage devices that use electronic circuits (like SSDs) rather than moving
    parts, as seen in traditional hard drives.
    >k ;;ajfha u;lh hkq iïm%odhsl oDV ;eáj, olakg ,efnk mßÈ p,kh jk fldgia j,g jvd
    bf,lafg%dksl mßm: (SSD jeks& Ndú;d lrk .nvd Wmdx. fõ'

    •    Impact on Size / m%udKh u; n,mEu

    Analysis / úYaf,aIKh
    The inventions that contributed most to reducing the size of computers are integrated circuits (ICs) and
    solid-state memory. These technologies allowed for a more compact design by replacing bulkier
    components with smaller, more efficient ones.

    Answer : 4 )

    Keywords Explanation / uQ, mo meyeÈ,s lsÍu

    CPU (Central Processing Unit / uOHu ieliqï tAllh)
    The main processing component of a computer that performs calculations and executes instructions. It
    often consists of several key units like the Arithmetic and Logic Unit (ALU), Control Unit (CU), and
    others.
    .Kkh lsÍï iy Wmfoia l%shd;aul lrk mß.Klhl m%Odk ieliqï ixrplh' th fndfyda úg wxl
    .Ks;h iy ;d¾lsl tAllh (ALU) md,k tAllh (CU) iy fjk;a m%Odk tAll lsysmhlska iukaú; fõ'

    Virtual Address / w;:H ,smskh
    This is the address used by a program to access memory. Virtual addresses are mapped to physical
    addresses by the system to allow efficient use of memory.
    u;lhg msúiSug jevigykla Ndú;d lrk ,smskh fuhhs' u;lh ld¾hlaIuj Ndú;d lsÍug bv
    i,id §u i|yd w;:H ,smsk moaO;sh uÕska fN!;sl ,smsk fj; is;shï .; lr we;'

    Physical Address / fN!;sl ,smskh
    The actual address in the computer's physical memory (RAM) where data is stored.
    o;a; .nvd lr we;s mß.Klfha fN!;sl u;lfha (RAM) ienE ,smskh fuhhs'

    Memory Translation Maps / u;l mßj¾;k is;shï
    These are data structures or tables maintained by the CPU to keep track of the mapping between virtual
    and physical addresses.
    fïjd w;:H iy fN!;sl ,smsk w;r is;shï.; lsÍu ksÍlaIKh lsÍu i|yd CPU úiska mj;ajdf.k
    hkq ,nk o;a; jHqyhka fyda j.= fõ'

    1) Arithmetic and Logic Unit / wxl .Ks;h iy ;d¾lsl tAllh (ALU)
       The ALU is the part of the CPU that handles all arithmetic operations (such as addition, subtraction,
       multiplication, division) and logical operations (like AND, OR, NOT).
       ALU hkq ish¨u wxl .Ks; fufyhqï ^tl;= lsÍu" wvq lsÍu" .=K lsÍu" fn§u jeks& iy ;d¾lsl
       fufyhqï AND,OR,NOT jeks& yiqrejk CPU ys fldgihs'
        It performs the computations required for executing instructions but does not deal with memory
        management or address translation.
        th Wmfoia l%shd;aul lsÍu i|yd wjYH .Kkh lsÍï isÿ lrk kuq;a u;l l<ukdlrKh fyda
        ,smsk mßj¾;kh iuÕ lghq;= fkdlrhs'
        The ALU works closely with other parts of the CPU to perform data processing but relies on the
        Memory Management Unit (MMU) for accessing data at specific memory addresses.
        ALU o;a; ieliSu isÿ lsÍu i|yd CPU ys wfkl=;a fldgia iuÕ iómj l%shd lrk kuq;a ksYaÑ;
        u;l ,smskj, o;a; fj; m%fõY ùu i|yd u;l l<ukdlrK tAllh MMU u; r|d mj;S'

    2) Control Unit / md,k tAllh (CU)
       The Control Unit directs and coordinates the operations within the CPU. It interprets the instructions
       from the program and signals the ALU, memory, and other parts of the CPU to perform actions.
       md,k tAllh CPU ;=< fufyhqï fufyhùu iy iïnkaëlrKh lrhs' th jevigyfka Wmfoia
       w¾:l:kh lrk w;r ALU u;lh iy CPU ys wfkl=;a fldgia l%shd lsÍug ix{d lrhs'
        It manages the execution of instructions but doesn’t handle the mapping of virtual addresses to
        physical addresses.
        th Wmfoia l%shd;aul lsÍu l<ukdlrKh lrk kuq;a fN!;sl ,smsk fj; w;:H ,smsk is;shï.;
        lsÍu yiqrejkafka ke;'
        Essentially, the Control Unit acts as a supervisor, guiding how the instructions are processed by the
        CPU components, but it doesn’t perform memory translation tasks.

    3) L1 Cache Memory / L1 jdrl u;lh
       L1 (Level 1) cache is a small, high-speed memory within or very close to the CPU, used to temporarily
       store data and instructions frequently accessed by the CPU. L1

       virtual addresses to physical addresses.

          Answer : 4 )
          Key Points of MCQ
          (i) L1 Cache / L1 jdrlh
              L1 cache, or Level 1 cache, is a small, high-speed memory located directly on the CPU chip, serving
              as the primary cache for data and instructions that the processor frequently accesses. It is the fastest
              type of cache memory, designed to minimize latency and improve processing speed by storing critical
              data close to the CPU. Typically, L1 cache is divided into two separate caches: one for instructions
              (L1i) and one for data (L1d), allowing for efficient retrieval and execution of both types of
              information. Each core in a multi-core processor has its own dedicated L1 cache, which is not shared
              with other cores, ensuring quick access to essential data without the delays associated with accessing
              slower memory types like DRAM.
              L1 jdrlh" fyda 1 jk uÜgfï jdrlh hkq ilikh ks;r m%fõY jk o;a; iy Wmfoia i|yd
              m%d:ñl jdrlh f,i l%shd lrk" CPU Ñmh u; iDcqju msysgd we;s l=vd" wêfõ.S u;lhls' th

    (ii) L2 Cache / L2 jdrlh
         L2 cache, or Level 2 cache, is a type of memory cache that acts as a secondary storage layer for data
         and instructions, positioned between the CPU's L1 cache and the main memory (RAM). It is typically
         larger than L1 cache, often ranging from 256KB to several megabytes, and is designed to provide a
         balance between speed and capacity, being slower than L1 but significantly faster than RAM. L2 cache
         can be embedded within the CPU chip or located on a separate chip, and in modern processors, it is
         usually dedicated to individual CPU cores, allowing each core to access its own L2 cache
         independently. This structure helps enhance overall system performance by reducing the time the CPU
         takes to retrieve frequently used data

    (iii) Main memory / m%Odk u;lh
          Main memory, commonly referred to as RAM (random access memory), is a crucial component of a
          computer system that temporarily stores data and instructions that the CPU needs for processing. It is
          a volatile memory type, meaning it loses its contents when power is turned off, and it allows for quick
          access to frequently used information, significantly enhancing overall system performance. Main
          memory is typically composed of dynamic RAM (DRAM), which is slower but more cost-effective,
          and static RAM (SRAM), which is faster but more expensive. The capacity of main memory directly
          impacts a computer's ability to run multiple applications simultaneously and manage larger datasets
          efficiently
          m%Odk u;lh" idudkHfhka RAM ^iiïNdù m%fõY u;lh& f,i y÷kajkq ,nk w;r" CPU yg

    (iv) Register / f¾csia;rh
         Registers are small, high-speed storage locations within a computer's CPU that temporarily hold data
         and instructions that are actively being processed. They enable the CPU to quickly access and
         manipulate data without the latency associated with accessing main memory (RAM). Registers can
         store various types of information, including instructions, memory addresses, and intermediate data,
         and are essential for performing arithmetic operations and controlling the flow of execution in
         programs. Each register is typically designed to hold a specific amount of data, such as 32 or 64 bits,
         depending on the architecture of the processor. Their speed and proximity to the CPU make them
         critical for efficient computing operations.
         frðia;rh hkq mß.Klfha CPU ;=< il%shj ielfiñka mj;sk o;a; iy Wmfoia ;djld,slj

    (v) Hard disk / oDV ;eáh
        A hard disk, or hard drive, is a non-volatile storage device that uses magnetic disks to permanently
        store large amounts of digital data, ranging from hundreds of gigabytes to tens of terabytes, making it
        a crucial component in most computers and other devices. It consists of one or more rigid platters
        coated with magnetic material, where data is stored in concentric tracks and sectors, and accessed by
        read/write heads in a random-access manner as the platters rotate at high speeds, allowing for fast
        retrieval of information, while retaining data even when the power is turned off, housed in a sealed
        metal case to protect the delicate components, providing a cost-effective solution for long-term storage
        compared to other media like optical discs, despite being susceptible to physical damage and
        generating heat and noise during operation.
        oDV ;eáhla fyda oDV ;eáhla hkq kYH fkdjk .nvd Wmdx.hla jk w;r th .s.dnhsÜ ish

    •   Capacity / Odß;dj
        This refers to the amount of data that each storage component can hold. Generally, larger storage
        components can store more data.
        fuh tla tla .nvd ixrpl ;nd .; yels o;a; m%udKhg fhduq fõ' idudkHfhka" úYd, .nvd

    •   Access Speed / m%fõY fõ.h

        speeds mean that the CPU can retrieve and process data more quickly.

    my; fþoh i,lkak'

     Answer : 5 )

     Key Points of MCQ

 (i) Program / l%uf,aLkh
     A program is a set of instructions written in a programming language that a computer can execute. These
     instructions are designed to perform a specific task or set of tasks, ranging from simple calculations to
     complex data processing. When a program is run, the computer's processor reads and executes these
     instructions sequentially or as directed by control flow commands.
     l%uf,aLkhla hkq mß.Klhlg l%shd;aul l< yels l%uf,aLk NdIdjlska ,shd we;s Wmfoia ud,djls'
     fuu Wmfoia ks¾udKh lr we;af;a ir, .Kkh lsÍï isg ixlS¾K o;a; ieliSu olajd ksYaÑ;
     ld¾hhla fyda ld¾hhka iuQyhla bgq lsÍug h' jevigykla l%shd;aul jk úg" mß.Klfha ilikh
     fuu Wmfoia wkql%ñlj fyda md,k m%jdy úOdk u.ska Wmfoia ,nd § lshjd l%shd;aul lrhs'

 (ii) CPU
      The Central Processing Unit (CPU) is the primary component of a computer that performs most of the
      processing tasks. It executes instructions from programs by performing basic arithmetic, logic, control,
      and input/output operations. The CPU acts as the brain of the computer, interpreting and carrying out
      instructions to ensure the smooth operation of programs and tasks.
      uOHu ieliqï tAllh ^CPU& hkq fndfyda ieliqï ld¾hhka bgq lrk mß.Klhl uQ,sl wx.hhs' th
      uQ,sl wxl .Ks;h" ;¾lkh" md,kh iy wdodk$m%;sodk fufyhqï isÿ lrñka jevigyka j,ska Wmfoia
      l%shd;aul lrhs' CPU mß.Klfha fud<h f,i l%shd lrhs" jevigyka iy ld¾hhka iqugj l%shd;aul
      ùu iy;sl lsÍu i|yd Wmfoia w¾: ksrEmKh lrhs'

 (iii) Program Counter /
       The Program Counter (PC) is a special register in the CPU that keeps track of the address of the next
       instruction to be executed. As instructions are processed, the PC is updated to point to the address of the
       following instruction. This ensures that the CPU can continue executing a program in the correct sequence,
       moving from one instruction to the next.
       jevigyka .Klh PC hkq CPU ys we;s úfYaI f,aLkhla jk w;r th l%shd;aul l< hq;= B<Õ
       Wmfoia j, ,smskh ksÍlaIKh lrhs' Wmfoia l%shdjg kxjk úg" my; Wmfoia j, ,smskh fj; fhduq
       lsÍug mß.Klh hdj;ald,Sk fõ' fuu.ska CPU yg ksjerÈ wkqms<sfj,ska jevigykla l%shd;aul
       lsÍu È.gu lrf.k hdug yels nj iy;sl lrhs'

 (iv) Memory Location / u;l ia:dk
      A memory location refers to a specific address in the computer's memory where data or instructions are
      stored. Each location has a unique address, allowing the CPU to access and retrieve the necessary
      information. Memory locations are used to store program code, variables, and intermediate results, which
      the CPU uses during the execution of a program.

 (v) Primary Storage / m%d:ñl wdphkh
     Primary storage, also known as main memory or RAM (Random Access Memory), is the computer's
     immediate, high-speed memory used to temporarily store data and instructions that are actively being used
     or processed by the CPU. Unlike secondary storage (like hard drives or SSDs), which retains data long-
     term, primary storage is volatile, meaning it loses its contents when the computer is turned off. It plays a
     crucial role in providing quick access to data and instructions, allowing for faster execution of programs
     and efficient multitasking.

 (vi) Secondary Storage / oaú;shsl wdphkh
      Secondary storage refers to non-volatile storage media used to save data and programs on a long-term
      basis. Unlike primary storage (RAM), secondary storage retains its contents even when the computer is
      powered off. Examples include hard drives (HDDs), solid-state drives (SSDs), optical discs (like CDs and
      DVDs), and external storage devices (like USB flash drives). Secondary storage is typically used for
      storing operating systems, applications, files, and other data that are not actively being processed but need
      to be preserved and accessed over time.

 (vii) Fetch execution cycle / bIag fijqï pl%h
       The fetch-execute cycle, also known as the fetch-decode-execute cycle, is the fundamental process
       through which a CPU executes instructions from a program. It consists of three main steps: fetch, where
       the CPU retrieves the next instruction from primary memory (RAM) based on the address in the program
       counter; decode, where the CPU interprets the fetched instruction to determine what action is needed;
       and execute, where the CPU performs the specified operation, such as arithmetic calculations or data
       movement. After execution, the cycle repeats with the program counter being updated to point to the next
       instruction, ensuring continuous processing of the program's code.
       bIag fijqï pl%h " fetch-decode-execute pl%h f,io ye¢kafõ" th CPU uÕska jevigyklska Wmfoia
       l%shd;aul lrk uQ,sl l%shdj,shhs' th m%Odk mshjr ;=klska iukaú; fõ( fetch" tys§ CPU úiska

       l%shd;aul lrkak" tys§ CPU úiska wxl .Ks; .Kkh lsÍï fyda o;a; p,kh jeks ksYaÑ; fufyhqu

          Key Points of MCQ
          (i) Software / uDÿldx.
                Software refers to the collection of programs, data, and instructions that enable a computer or other
                electronic devices to perform specific tasks. Unlike hardware, which includes the physical components
                of a computer system, software is intangible and consists of lines of code written by programmers. It
                acts as the intermediary between users and hardware, translating user inputs into commands that the
                hardware executes.
                uDÿldx. hkq mß.Klhlg fyda fjk;a bf,lafg%dksl Wmdx.j,g ksYaÑ; ld¾hhka bgq lsÍug

                oDvdx. fuka fkdj" uDÿldx. wiamDYH jk w;r l%uf,aLlhska úiska ,shk ,o fla; f¾Ld j,ska
                iukaú; fõ' th mßYS,lhka iy oDVdx. w;r w;rueÈhd f,i l%shd lrhs" oDVdx. l%shd;aul
                lrk úOdkj,g mßYS,l fhojqï mßj¾;kh lrhs'

                Software can be broadly categorized into two types: system software, which includes operating
                systems and utility programs essential for managing hardware and system operations, and application
                software, which performs specific functions for users, such as word processing, web browsing, or
                gaming. Software can be distributed and licensed in various ways, including proprietary licenses that
                restrict its use and distribution, and open-source licenses that allow users to freely access, modify, and
                distribute the source code.

    (ii) Application software / fhÿï uDÿldx.
         Application software is a category of programs designed to help users accomplish specific tasks or
         solve particular problems. Unlike system software, which manages hardware and provides a platform
         for other software, application software is focused on end-user needs and interacts directly with users
         to facilitate activities such as document creation, data analysis, web browsing, and communication.
         Examples include productivity tools like Microsoft Word for writing documents, web browsers like
         Google Chrome for internet navigation, and multimedia applications like VLC Media Player for
         playing audio and video files. These programs are crafted to be intuitive and user-friendly, making it
         easier for individuals to perform a wide range of activities, from managing personal finances to
         designing graphics, thereby enhancing both efficiency and creativity in daily tasks.
         fhÿï uDÿldx. hkq mßYS,lhskag ksYaÑ; ld¾hhka bgq lsÍug fyda úfYaIs; .eg¿ úi£ug

    (iii) Open-source software / újD; uQ,dY% uDÿldx.
          Open-source software is a type of software whose source code is made available to the public to view,
          modify, and distribute. This transparency allows anyone to inspect, enhance, or customize the software
          according to their needs. Unlike proprietary software, which is controlled by a single entity and
          restricts access to its underlying code, open-source software fosters a collaborative development
          environment. It is typically released under licenses that comply with open-source standards, such as
          the GNU General Public License (GPL) or the Apache License, which ensure that the software remains
          freely available and that modifications are also shared with the community. Popular examples of open-
          source software include the Linux operating system, the Mozilla Firefox web browser, and the Apache
          HTTP Server.

    (iv) Utility software Wmfhda.s;d uDÿldx.
         Utility software refers to a range of programs designed to manage, maintain, and optimize a computer
         system’s performance. Unlike application software, which helps users accomplish specific tasks,
         utility software focuses on the system's upkeep and efficiency. It includes tools such as antivirus
         programs that protect against malware, disk cleanup utilities that remove unnecessary files to free up
         storage space, and backup software that ensures important data is securely copied and can be restored
         if needed. Additionally, it encompasses file management tools for organizing and compressing files,
         as well as system monitoring tools that provide insights into the computer's performance and resource
         usage. By performing these critical functions, utility software helps ensure that a computer runs
         smoothly, securely, and efficiently over time.

    (v) Proprietary software / ysñlï wdY%s; uDÿldx.
        Proprietary software refers to software that is owned by an individual or a company, and its source
        code is kept confidential. Unlike open-source software, which allows anyone to view, modify, and
        distribute the code, proprietary software restricts access to the underlying code and is distributed under
        strict licensing agreements. Users must typically purchase a license to use the software, and the terms
        of use, including restrictions on copying, modifying, or redistributing, are controlled by the software’s
        owner. Examples include Microsoft Windows and Adobe Photoshop. Proprietary software often
        provides a structured support system and regular updates from the vendor, but it also limits user control
        over customization and innovation compared to open-source alternatives.
        ysñlï wdY%s; uDÿldx. hkq mqoa.,fhl=g fyda iud.ulg wh;a uDÿldx.hla jk w;r tys uQ,

        úl,amhkag idfmalaIj wNsreÑlrKh iy kfjda;amdokh ms<sn| mßYS,l md,kh iSud lrhs'

    (vi) Systems software / moaO;s uDÿldx.
         System software is a category of software designed to manage and control a computer's hardware and
         provide a platform for running application software. It acts as an intermediary between the user and
         the hardware, ensuring that the computer's hardware components function correctly and efficiently.
         The primary component of system software is the operating system, such as Windows, macOS, or
         Linux, which oversees essential functions like managing memory, processing tasks, and handling input
         and output operations. System software also includes utility programs that perform maintenance tasks,
         such as disk management tools and antivirus software. By facilitating communication between
         hardware and application software, system software plays a crucial role in enabling the overall
         functionality and performance of a computer system.
         moaO;s uDÿldx. hkq mß.Klhl oDvdx. l<ukdlrKh lsÍug iy md,kh lsÍug iy fhÿï

         ld¾hNdrhla bgq lrhs'

    Proprietary software is owned by an individual or a company, and its source code is typically not available
    to the public. Users must purchase a license to use it, and the use and distribution of the software are
    controlled by the owner.
    ysñlï wdY%s; uDÿldx. mqoa.,fhl=g fyda iud.ulg wh;a jk w;r" tys uQ, fla;h idudkHfhka
    uyckhdg ,nd .; fkdyel' mßYS,lhka th Ndú;d lsÍug n,m;arhla ñ,§ .; hq;= w;r" uDÿldx.
    Ndú;h iy fnod yeÍu whs;slre úiska md,kh lrkq ,efí'

    Open-source software, on the other hand, is released with a license that allows anyone to view, modify,
    and distribute its source code. It is not owned in the traditional sense, and its licensing model is designed
    to promote collaboration and sharing.
    újD; uQ,dY% uDÿldx." wfkla w;g" ´kEu flfkl=g tys m%Nj fla;h ne,Sug" fjkia lsÍug iy

    (1) Application software and open-source software
        fhÿï (application) uDÿldx. iy újD; uQ,dY% (open-source) uDÿldx.
        Application software refers to any software designed to help the user perform specific tasks (e.g., word
        processors, games), and it can be either proprietary or open-source.

    Answer : 4 )
    Key Points of MCQ
    (i) L1 Cache / L1 jdrlh
        L1 cache, or Level 1 cache, is a small, high-speed memory located directly on the CPU chip, serving
        as the primary cache for data and instructions that the processor frequently accesses. It is the fastest
        type of cache memory, designed to minimize latency and improve processing speed by storing critical
        data close to the CPU. Typically, L1 cache is divided into two separate caches: one for instructions
        (L1i) and one for data (L1d), allowing for efficient retrieval and execution of both types of
        information. Each core in a multi-core processor has its own dedicated L1 cache, which is not shared
        with other cores, ensuring quick access to essential data without the delays associated with accessing
        slower memory types like DRAM.
        L1 jdrlh" fyda 1 jk uÜgfï jdrlh hkq ilikh ks;r m%fõY jk o;a; iy Wmfoia i|yd
        m%d:ñl jdrlh f,i l%shd lrk" CPU Ñmh u; iDcqju msysgd we;s l=vd" wêfõ.S u;lhls' th
        CPU fj; iómj ;SrKd;aul o;a; .nvd lsÍu u.ska m%udoh wju lsÍug iy ieliqï fõ.h
        jeä ÈhqKq lsÍug ks¾udKh lr we;s fõ.j;au jdrl u;lh fõ' idudkHfhka" L1 jdrlh fjku
        jdrl follg fnod we;( L1 tlla Wmfoia i|yd iy L1 tlla o;a; i|yd" f;dr;=re j¾. folu
        ld¾hlaIuj ,nd .ekSug iy l%shd;aul lsÍug bv i,ihs' nyq-yr ilikhl iEu yrhlgu
        ;ukaf.au lemjQ L1 jdrl we;" th wfkl=;a uOHhka iuÕ fnod fkd.kS" DRAM jeks ukao.dó
        u;l j¾.j,g m%fõY ùu yd iïnkaO m%udohlska f;drj w;HjYH o;a; fj; blauka m%fõYh
        iy;sl lrhs'

RAVINDU BANDARANAYAKE                                -67-                                          #ictfromabc

    (ii) L2 Cache / L2 jdrlh
         L2 cache, or Level 2 cache, is a type of memory cache that acts as a secondary storage layer for data
         and instructions, positioned between the CPU's L1 cache and the main memory (RAM). It is typically
         larger than L1 cache, often ranging from 256KB to several megabytes, and is designed to provide a
         balance between speed and capacity, being slower than L1 but significantly faster than RAM. L2 cache
         can be embedded within the CPU chip or located on a separate chip, and in modern processors, it is
         usually dedicated to individual CPU cores, allowing each core to access its own L2 cache
         independently. This structure helps enhance overall system performance by reducing the time the CPU
         takes to retrieve frequently used data
         L2 jdrlh" fyda 2 jk uÜgfï jdrlh hkq CPU ys L1 jdrlh iy m%Odk u;lh RAM w;r

         .;jk ld,h wvq lsÍfuka iuia; moaO;sfha l%shdldß;ajh jeä ÈhqKq lsÍug WmldÍ fõ.

    (iii) Main memory / m%Odk u;lh
          Main memory, commonly referred to as RAM (random access memory), is a crucial component of a
          computer system that temporarily stores data and instructions that the CPU needs for processing. It is
          a volatile memory type, meaning it loses its contents when power is turned off, and it allows for quick
          access to frequently used information, significantly enhancing overall system performance. Main
          memory is typically composed of dynamic RAM (DRAM), which is slower but more cost-effective,
          and static RAM (SRAM), which is faster but more expensive. The capacity of main memory directly
          impacts a computer's ability to run multiple applications simultaneously and manage larger datasets
          efficiently
          m%Odk u;lh" idudkHfhka RAM ^iiïNdù m%fõY u;lh& f,i y÷kajkq ,nk w;r" CPU yg

    (iv) Register / f¾csia;rh
         Registers are small, high-speed storage locations within a computer's CPU that temporarily hold data
         and instructions that are actively being processed. They enable the CPU to quickly access and
         manipulate data without the latency associated with accessing main memory (RAM). Registers can
         store various types of information, including instructions, memory addresses, and intermediate data,
         and are essential for performing arithmetic operations and controlling the flow of execution in
         programs. Each register is typically designed to hold a specific amount of data, such as 32 or 64 bits,
         depending on the architecture of the processor. Their speed and proximity to the CPU make them
         critical for efficient computing operations.
         frðia;rh hkq mß.Klfha CPU ;=< il%shj ielfiñka mj;sk o;a; iy Wmfoia ;djld,slj
         r|jd ;nd .kakd l=vd" wêfõ.S .nvd ia:dk fõ' m%Odk u;lhg RAM m%fõY ùu yd iïnkaO

RAVINDU BANDARANAYAKE                               -68-                                           #ictfromabc

    (v) Hard disk / oDV ;eáh
        A hard disk, or hard drive, is a non-volatile storage device that uses magnetic disks to permanently
        store large amounts of digital data, ranging from hundreds of gigabytes to tens of terabytes, making it
        a crucial component in most computers and other devices. It consists of one or more rigid platters
        coated with magnetic material, where data is stored in concentric tracks and sectors, and accessed by
        read/write heads in a random-access manner as the platters rotate at high speeds, allowing for fast
        retrieval of information, while retaining data even when the power is turned off, housed in a sealed
        metal case to protect the delicate components, providing a cost-effective solution for long-term storage
        compared to other media like optical discs, despite being susceptible to physical damage and
        generating heat and noise during operation.
        oDV ;eáhla fyda oDV ;eáhla hkq kYH fkdjk .nvd Wmdx.hla jk w;r th .s.dnhsÜ ish

        w;r;=r ;dmh yd Yíoh W;amdokh lsÍu

       •   Capacity / Odß;dj
           This refers to the amount of data that each storage component can hold. Generally, larger storage
           components can store more data.
           fuh tla tla .nvd ixrpl ;nd .; yels o;a; m%udKhg fhduq fõ' idudkHfhka" úYd, .nvd
           ixrpl j,g jeä o;a; .nvd l< yel

       •   Access Speed / m%fõY fõ.h
           This indicates how quickly data can be read from or written to the storage component. Faster access
           speeds mean that the CPU can retrieve and process data more quickly.

    Dot Matrix Printers - ;s;a kHdi uqøK hka;%h

    How It Works - tys l%shdldÍ;ajh
    1    Print Head and Ink Ribbon - uqøK YS¾Ih iy ;Ska; mS;a; máh
         A dot matrix printer uses a print head that contains a series of pins arranged in a matrix. The print
         head moves horizontally across the paper while the pins strike an ink ribbon. This impact transfers ink
         from the ribbon onto the paper in the form of dots. By combining these dots, the printer creates letters,
         numbers, and images.
         ;s;a kHdi uqøK hka;%h kHdihl ilik ,o lgq ud,djla wvx.= uqøK YS¾Ihla Ndú;d lrhs' uqøK

    2    Paper Movement - lvodisfha p,kh

    Inkjet Printers - ;Ska; úÿï uqøK hka;%h

    How It Works - tys l%shdldÍ;ajh
    1. Spraying Ink - ;Ska; biSu
       Inkjet printers use a print head equipped with tiny nozzles that spray microscopic droplets of ink
       directly onto the paper. The ink is deposited in a precise pattern to create text and images. This method
       allows for high-resolution and vibrant color prints.
       ;Ska; úÿï uqøK hka;% l=vd ;=vq j,ska iukaú; uqøK YS¾Ihla Ndú;d lrk w;r tuÕska ;Ska;j,

    Laser Printers - f,ai¾ uqøK hka;%h

    How It Works - tys l%shdldÍ;ajh
    1. Laser and Drum - f,ai¾ iy fnrh
       Laser printers use a laser beam to write an image onto a rotating drum (also known as a photoconductor
       or imaging drum). The laser creates an electrostatic charge on the drum's surface in the pattern of the
       image.
       f,ai¾ uqøK hka;% uÕska N%uKh jk fnrhla u; rEmhla ,sùug f,ai¾ loïNhla Ndú;d lrhs
       ^m%ldY ikakdhl fyda m%ldY fnrh f,io ye¢kafõ&' f,ai¾ rEmfha rgdj wkqj fnrfha u;=msg
       úoHq;a iaÓ;sl wdfrdamKhla ks¾udKh lrhs'

    2. Toner Transfer - fgdak¾ udre lsÍu
       Toner, which is a fine powder, is attracted to the charged areas of the drum. The toner then gets
       transferred from the drum to the paper. The paper is heated (fused) to bond the toner to the paper,
       producing the final print.
       isyska l=vq j¾.hla jk fgdak¾ fnrfha wdfrdams; m%foaYj,g wdl¾IKh fõ' túg fgdak¾ fnrfha
       isg lvodis fj; udre fõ' fgdak¾ lvodishg nkaOkh lsÍu i|yd lvodis r;a lr ^ú,Sk lr&
       wjika uqøKh ksmojhs'

    3. Paper Path
       The paper moves through the printer, where it picks up the toner from the drum and passes through
       the fuser.
       lvodis uqøK hka;%h yryd .uka lrhs" tys§ th fnrfhka fgdakrh ,ndf.k *shqirh yryd
       .uka lrhs'

RAVINDU BANDARANAYAKE                                -76-                                           #ictfromabc

    Answer : 3 )

    The northbridge is responsible for handling high-speed components, such as the CPU, memory (RAM),
    ROM and transferring data between hard disk and RAM. Located close to the CPU on the motherboard,
    the northbridge uses a high-speed bus for direct data transfer. In modern systems, many of the northbridge's
    functions, like memory and ROM communication, have been integrated into the CPU itself.
    CPU" u;lh (RAM), ROM jeks wêfõ.S Wmdx. yeisrùu iy oDV äialh iy RAM w;r o;a;
    yqjudre lsÍu i|yd Northbridge j.lSu orhs' ujq mqjrefõ CPU tlg wdikakj msysgd we;s
    Northbridge iDcq o;a; yqjudrej i|yd wêfõ.S nihla Ndú;d lrhs' kùk moaO;s ;=<" u;lh iy
    ROM ikaksfõokh jeks Northbridge ys fndfyda ld¾hhka CPU fj;u tAldnoaO lr we;'

    The southbridge, on the other hand, manages slower components and peripherals. These include USB
    ports, audio ports, network interfaces, and storage devices like hard drives and solid-state drives. It serves
    as the controller for input/output (I/O) operations and peripherals, ensuring these devices communicate
    effectively with the CPU. Unlike the northbridge, the southbridge is located further away from the CPU
    as it handles lower-speed tasks. In newer systems, the southbridge’s functionality has been combined into
    a single chip known as the Platform Controller Hub (PCH).
    wfkla w;g" southbridge ukao.dó ixrpl iy m¾hka; l<ukdlrKh lrhs' fïjdg USB flfjks"
    Y%jH flfjks" cd, w;=reuqyqK;a iy ùäfhda flfjks we;=<;a fõ' th wdodk$m%;sodk (I/O) fufyhqï
    iy m¾hka; i|yd md,lh f,i l%shd lrhs" fuu Wmdx. CPU iuÕ M,odhS f,i ikaksfõokh lsÍu
    iy;sl lrhs' northbridge fuka fkdj" southbridge wvq fõ. ld¾hhka yiqrejk neúka CPU fj;ska
    ;j;a ÿßka msysgd we;'

    Together, the northbridge and southbridge work to facilitate smooth data flow across all components of
    the computer, ensuring efficient system performance.
    northbridge iy southbridge tlaj" ld¾hlaIu moaO;s l%shdldß;ajh iy;sl lrñka mß.Klfha ish¨u
    wx. yryd iqug o;a; m%jdyhg myiqlï ie,iSug l%shd lrhs'

    Accordingly, / tA wkqj"

    X – CPU
    Y – memory / u;lh
    Z – connector for video port / ùäfhda flfjksh i|yd iïnkaOlh

    So, the correct answer number here is 3.
    tuksid, fuys ksjerÈ ms<s;=re wxlh 3 fõ.

    1) Decreasing RAM and reinstalling the operating system

    Answer : 5 )

    Booting
    The process of starting up a computer and loading the operating system into memory from a storage device
    (ex: hard disk or SSD). Faster booting means the system starts more quickly after being turned on.
    mß.Klhla wdrïN lsÍu iy .nvd Wmdx.hlska fufyhqï moaO;sh u;lhg megùfï l%shdj,sh ^Wod(
    oDV ;eáh fyda SSD&' fõ.j;a wdrïNhla hkq moaO;sh il%sh lsÍfuka miq blaukska wdrïN ùuhs'

    Hard Disk / oDV ;eáh
    A traditional data storage device (HDD) using spinning magnetic platters to store and retrieve digital
    information. It is slower compared to modern storage technologies like SSDs.
    wxls; f;dr;=re .nvd lsÍu iy ,nd .ekSu i|yd lerflk pqïnl ;eá Ndú;d lrk iïm%odhsl
    o;a; .nvd lsÍfï Wmdx.hls (HDD) SSD jeks kùk .nvd ;dlaIKhkag idfmalaIj ukao.dó fõ'

    RAM (Random Access Memory / iiïNdú m%fõY u;lh)
    Temporary memory used by the computer to perform tasks. More RAM generally means better
    multitasking and faster performance, but it doesn’t directly affect boot speed.
    ;djld,sl u;lh ld¾hhka bgq lsÍu i|yd mß.Klh Ndú;d lrhs' jeä RAM hkq idudkHfhka jvd
    fyd| nyq-ld¾hhla iy fõ.j;a l%shdldß;ajhla woyia lrhs' kuq;a th werUqï fõ.h fl,skau
    n,mdkafka ke;'

    Formatting / wdlD;slrK
    The process of preparing a storage device like a hard disk by erasing all data and setting up a new file
    system. It doesn’t inherently improve speed unless combined with other actions.
    ish¨u o;a; uld oud kj f.dkq moaO;shla ieliSfuka oDV ;eáhla jeks .nvd Wmdx.hla ilia lsÍfï
    l%shdj,shhs' th fjk;a l%shd iuÕ ixl,kh ù we;akï ñi iycfhkau fõ.h jeä ÈhqKq fkdlrhs'

    CD Drive
    An optical drive used for reading or writing CDs. Installing a new CD drive has no impact on boot speed.
    ixhqla; ;eá lshùu fyda ,sùu i|yd Ndú;d lrk oDYH Odjlhla' kj CD Odjlhla ia:dmkh lsÍu
    werUqï fõ.h flfrys n,mEula ke;'

    Fan / úÿ,s mxldj
    A cooling component that prevents the computer’s internal components from overheating. Replacing a fan
    addresses thermal issues but doesn’t affect speed.
    mß.Klfha wNHka;r fldgia Wkqiqï ùu j<lajk isis,k ixrplhls' úÿ,s mxldjla m%;sia:dmkh
    lsÍfuka ;dm .eg¿ úi|k kuq;a fõ.hg n,mdkafka ke;'

    Solid-State Drive / >k-;;aj Odjl (SSD)
    A modern storage device that uses flash memory. It is much faster than traditional hard disks (HDDs) for
    reading and writing data.
    iefK,s u;lh Ndú;d lrk kùk .nvd Wmdx.hls' o;a; lshùu iy ,sùu i|yd iïm%odhsl oDV
    ;eá (HDDs) j,g jvd th b;d fõ.j;a fõ'

    l%shdldÍ;ajhg n,mdkafka ke;'

    contributing to quicker boot times.

    odhl fõ'
